=== page 3 ===
Travel Reimagined 
with AI
Few industries are as well suited to this 
transformation as travel. Every journey is a 
sequence of decisions, shaped by changing 
prices, fragmented inventory, evolving 
itineraries and unexpected disruptions. 
AI gives us the ability to connect these 
decisions through continuous intelligence, 
creating travel experiences that are more 
intuitive, seamless, contextual and responsive 
from start to finish.
For ixigo, this is not about adding AI to 
an existing travel platform. It is about 
fundamentally reimagining how travel can be 
experienced. With an AI-native architecture, 
proprietary travel data, multimodal AI and 
agentic capabilities, intelligence is becoming 
a foundational layer across the travel lifecycle — from discovery and booking 
to assistance, fulfilment and post-trip 
engagement.
Our ambition is to move from simply helping 
travellers make decisions to increasingly 
helping them execute and manage their 
journeys. AI can understand context, 
anticipate needs, personalise experiences 
and take action, while also powering 
smarter pricing, recommendations, faster 
issue resolution, autonomous service and 
greater operational efficiency.
This is what we mean by Travel Reimagined 
with AI: a travel platform that becomes 
more intelligent, responsive and valuable 
with every interaction.Every technological shift improves efficiency and changes how 
industries operate. Artificial intelligence is doing something more 
profound. It is embedding intelligence into every customer interaction, 
enabling businesses to anticipate needs, personalise experiences and 
act with unprecedented speed and precision.

About Us
The ixigo Ecosystem
We are India’s leading AI-native travel platform, helping millions of travellers 
every day to plan, book and manage their trips across rail, air, buses and 
hotels through our ixigo, Confirmtkt and AbhiBus apps. With over 57 crore 
Annual Active Users in Fiscal 2026, ixigo is India’s leading travel platform by 
user base.
Listed On
NSE: IXIGO • BSE: 544192Vision
Our vision is to become the most customer 
centric travel company, by offering the best 
customer experience to our users.

Travel Reimagined with AI
Where Intelligence Meets Mobility
Travel is one of India’s most fragmented consumer categories, shaped 
by dynamic pricing, fluctuating inventory and frequent operational 
disruptions. We believe artificial intelligence is transforming how 
travellers navigate this complexity, enabling smarter decisions at every 
stage of the journey.
Our focus on customer experience has guided 
us since inception. While our purpose has 
stayed the same, the technology available to 
fulfil it has advanced significantly. We began 
by developing predictive models for waitlist 
confirmations and fare recommendations. 
Artificial intelligence is embedded throughout our platform, enriching 
customer experiences and business performance. Our investment 
strategy is centred on three priorities:
Our
StrategyAI
Today, we combine proprietary travel intelligence 
with large language and voice models to create 
experiences that are more intuitive, personalised 
and proactive. As AI capabilities evolve, we see 
them becoming integral to how travellers plan, 
book and manage every journey.
helping travellers 
navigate disruptions
unlocking new revenue 
opportunities
improving productivity 
through intelligent 
automation

=== page 11 ===
ixigo NEXT
The AI-Native Future of Travel
ixigo NEXT is our most significant product initiative of the year. We established 
a dedicated team to redesign our technology architecture around AI, making 
our systems AI-readable, our APIs AI-accessible and our workflows AI-
compatible. This goes beyond layering AI features onto an existing travel 
platform; it represents a fundamental shift in how ixigo builds, operates 
and evolves its technology. Redesigning the underlying technology stack 
has shortened development cycles, enabling capabilities that previously 
required years of development to be delivered within months, while creating a 
foundation for greater speed, scalability and operating leverage.
ixigo NEXT is the result of this transformation. Rather than operating as a standalone assistant, it serves as a unified 
intelligence layer across the travel journey, connecting AI models, proprietary travel intelligence, real-time travel data 
and transactional capabilities. It combines advanced large language and voice models with nearly two decades of 
proprietary travel intelligence.
The platform brings together traveller intent, context and real-time travel information to create a continuous 
intelligence layer across the journey. Every interaction creates additional context, enabling the platform to become 
increasingly relevant, responsive and personalised over time.
ixigo NEXT reimagines the traditional travel journey 
across three stages: helping travellers discover 
possibilities, make better decisions and increasingly 
act on their behalf. What begins as conversational 
discovery can extend into personalised 
recommendations, booking, journey management 
and agentic actions - bringing more of the travel 
journey into a single intelligent experience.Conversational Travel Experience
The AI-native ixigo app replaces 
traditional search journeys with 
conversational interactions. Instead of 
navigating multiple filters and screens, 
travellers can express their travel 
requirements naturally through voice, 
text or tap to discover and book flights 
and hotels.
This allows travellers to interact with 
ixigo in the way that feels most natural to 
them, rather than adapting themselves to 
rigid search interfaces. The experience is 
designed to understand intent, context 
and preferences, including when a 
traveller’s request does not resemble a 
conventional travel search.Decide 
Discover
Act
Annual Report 2025-26 Financial Statements Statutory Reports Corporate Overview

=== page 12 ===
TARA
At the centre of this experience 
is TARA, ixigo's multimodal AI 
assistant. Reinvented as the 
conversational core of the ixigo 
app, TARA is deeply embedded 
into the core user experience 
rather than operating as a 
standalone chatbot. TARA is 
embedded into the app's core 
experience, understanding 
traveller intent and providing 
personalised recommendations 
based on travel history, 
preferences and the user's stage 
in the journey.
TARA enables travellers to interact 
naturally through voice, text and tap, 
with support across English, Hindi and 
Hinglish. It can understand complex, 
intent-driven queries, plan trips and 
surface relevant recommendations and 
actions based on the traveller's context.
Importantly, TARA can understand 
intent beyond explicit travel queries. 
By combining conversational context 
with travel history, preferences and 
past booking behaviour, it can move 
from simply responding to searches to 
anticipating what a traveller may  
need next.Trip Mode: Intelligence 
Throughout the Journey
ixigo's AI-native app also introduces Trip 
Mode, a built-in travel companion that 
extends the role of ixigo beyond booking 
and into the journey itself.
Trip Mode consolidates all post-booking 
information into a single view, including 
boarding passes, terminal and gate 
details, boarding information, baggage 
belt updates and other real-time travel 
alerts. It brings together the information 
a traveller needs at each stage of the 
journey, while using real-time context to 
surface relevant assistance.
The feature also helps users prepare 
for every stage of their journey, from 
planning and packing to boarding and 
arrival. It offers contextual assistance 
such as suggesting when to leave 
for the airport based on live traffic conditions, while providing relevant 
recommendations throughout the trip. 
Users can also receive personalised 
content suggestions, including 
recommendations on what to watch, eat 
or explore during their journey.
Trip Mode represents a shift from ixigo 
as a transaction platform to ixigo as 
a journey companion, ensuring that 
intelligence continues to assist travellers 
after a booking has been completed.
Agentic Travel Flows: From 
Assistance to Action
This year also marked the introduction of 
agentic travel experiences, representing 
the next stage in the evolution of AI at 
ixigo — from helping travellers search 
and decide to increasingly helping them 
execute and manage travel tasks.
Annual Report 2025-26 Financial Statements Statutory Reports Corporate Overview
08 09
=== page 13 ===
ixigo’s AI agents perform selected travel 
tasks autonomously within defined 
permissions and with user consent. 
These agents monitor flight schedules, 
notify travellers of delays or schedule 
changes, assist with refund processing 
following cancellations and support 
post-booking travel management.
ixigo’s AI agents also send boarding 
passes directly to travellers on 
WhatsApp, while giving them the option 
to add them to Apple Wallet and Google 
Wallet, and share them with Digi Yatra 
for a more seamless airport experience. 
Boarding passes stored in digital wallets 
can be dynamically updated in real time 
if there are changes to flight timings or 
gate information.
Beyond flights, ixigo’s AI agents 
proactively assist hotel travellers by 
coordinating with hotels ahead of check-in to verify room readiness, 
helping extend the intelligence layer 
beyond the booking transaction and 
into the stay itself.
These agentic experiences are designed 
around user control. Permission-based 
and opt-in flows ensure that travellers 
remain in control of when AI can take 
action on their behalf, while the scope of 
agentic capabilities continues to expand.
Travel Intelligence
ixigo combines nearly two decades 
of proprietary travel intelligence and 
traveller interactions with real-time 
information spanning flights, trains 
and buses, including live availability, 
disruptions, cancellations, pricing, 
proprietary recommendation and 
prediction engines, and other contextual 
travel signals.
Inside ixigo NEXTThis proprietary travel intelligence 
provides the context that makes AI 
useful for a complex category such 
as travel. While AI models provide 
the intelligence layer, ixigo’s deep 
understanding of traveller behaviour, 
travel inventory and real-time journey 
conditions enables that intelligence to 
be applied in a highly contextual way.
ixigo proactively monitors travel 
disruptions, provides real-time journey 
updates and supports travellers 
before, during and after their trips. 
By continuously combining traveller 
context with live travel information, 
ixigo can move from reactive 
assistance towards more proactive and 
personalised experiences.

=== page 15 ===
AI Driving Business Outcomes 
From Intelligence to Impact
Every journey generates thousands of pricing decisions, customer interactions 
and operational events. We apply artificial intelligence throughout these 
workflows to improve decision-making, automate high-volume processes and 
enhance productivity as transaction volumes grow.
AI powers dynamic pricing for our Peace of Mind 
portfolio, strengthens post-booking assistance 
and automates disruption management through 
solutions such as ixigo Assured, Price Lock, Abhi 
Assured, Assured Flex, Alternate Travel Plan and 
Travel Guarantee. 
Within our operations, AI handled 12.71 million 
customer queries, achieving resolution rates of 
90.03%. By automating a growing share of routine 
customer interactions, AI enables our teams to 
focus on more complex and high-value customer 
needs while supporting service at scale.
These AI-enabled productivity gains are 
contributing to greater operating leverage as 
the business scales, with annualised revenue per 
employee reaching approximately H 2 crore.      
Data Privacy and Cybersecurity
During FY26, Indian Railways introduced stricter anti-bot controls and mandatory 
Aadhaar-based user verification. We upgraded our platform to align with the revised 
authentication framework and completed the re-verification of a significant portion of 
our existing train booking user base.
As AI became more deeply integrated into booking, payments and customer 
authentication, we further strengthened our technology infrastructure to support secure, 
reliable and compliant platform operations.

=== page 17 ===
FY26 at a Glance
Turning Vision into Value
The year’s financial performance was characterised by strong revenue growth 
and cash generation. Revenue from operations increased by 34%, while 
Contribution Margin grew by 18% despite moderation in margin percentages 
across the portfolio. Operating cash flow increased by 60% and our preferential 
equity raise strengthened the balance sheet, providing greater flexibility for 
future investments.
We also made significant progress in strengthening our technology platform and creating new avenues for growth. We advanced 
our AI-native transformation through ixigo NEXT, extended AI capabilities across the Group, and expanded internationally through 
the acquisition of a 60% stake in Trenes.com, Spain’s second-largest train OTA. We broadened our multimodal travel ecosystem 
across flights, trains, buses, hotels and airport mobility, while forging new partnerships and distribution integrations that expanded 
our reach and strengthened the connected travel experience for our customers.

=== page 19 ===
We strengthened our hotels business through the acquisition of a 54.66% majority 
stake in Brevistay, India’s largest flexible-stay hotel network, expanding our 
presence in the flexible and short-stay accommodation segment. Following the 
acquisition, our direct hotel footprint expanded to 10,000+ properties across 700 
towns in India, powered by our AI-first hotel extranet, HELLO. Overall hotel supply 
also crossed 70,000 properties across India and 1 million properties worldwide, 
significantly broadening the depth and reach of our accommodation offering.
We invested J 75 million in Ofintelligence Technologies Private Limited (ProactAI) for 
a 10.34% stake, expanding our investments in emerging AI technologies with potential 
applications across the travel ecosystem. Its computer-vision capabilities can enable 
real-time intelligence across airports, hotels, bus terminals and railway stations, including 
passenger-flow analytics, queue management, staff optimisation and safety monitoring.  
The investment complements our broader strategy of extending AI-led innovation beyond 
the customer interface into travel infrastructure and operations.
We subscribed to 450,000 fully convertible debentures of Forgeurai Systems Private 
Limited (Vestra.AI) for J  45 million, further expanding our investments in AI-focused 
technology capabilities. Vestra.AI is building an AI-native workspace that brings together 
AI agents capable of executing tasks end-to-end, supported by a context engine designed 
to create a continuously evolving organisational knowledge layer. The investment 
complements our broader strategy of identifying and supporting technologies that can 
accelerate AI-led products, automation and operational capabilities.     We also took a significant step in our international expansion with the acquisition 
of a 60% stake in Trenes.com, Spain’s second-largest train OTA. Our first major 
international acquisition, the investment marks ixigo’s entry into Europe and brings 
together Trenes’ strong customer base, local rail operator integrations and market 
expertise with ixigo’s AI-led product innovation and technology capabilities. The 
acquisition creates an opportunity to accelerate product development and AI-led 
reinvention while expanding our ability to solve complex rail travel use cases at scale.    
Alongside the acquisition of Trenes, we acquired a 45.02% stake in Squad 
As A Service, S.L. (Sqaas), a Spain-based AI technology company. As a 
result, Sqaas became an associate company of ixigo. The company develops 
technology-driven solutions, with a focus on AI-powered software and 
related services.
Prosus (MIH Investments One B.V.) invested H  12,955.63 million 
in ixigo through a preferential issue of equity shares, acquiring a 
10.1% stake on a fully diluted basis. The investment strengthened 
our balance sheet and enhanced our financial flexibility to support 
our next phase of growth, with the funds allocated towards AI and 
technology investments, expanding our hotels business, selective 
inorganic opportunities and working capital requirements.    Strategic DevelopmentsAnnual Report 2025-26 Financial Statements Statutory Reports Corporate Overview

=== page 20 ===
Partnerships & Ecosystem
We deepened our collaboration 
with OpenAI to advance and scale 
artificial intelligence capabilities 
across ixigo, Confirmtkt and 
AbhiBus. We leveraged OpenAI’s 
Enterprise API platforms and 
next-generation coding models 
across our technology stack, 
supporting the development of 
autonomous workflows and new 
AI-driven use cases. These include 
fully autonomous agents, AI-
assisted workflows and contextual 
AI assistants, while supporting 
continued research into AI-first 
travel experiences.
We partnered with CRED to exclusively power flight 
bookings for the newly launched CRED IndusInd 
Bank RuPay Credit Card. ixigo’s flight inventory and 
technology powered the booking experience within the 
CRED ecosystem, enabling cardholders to book flights 
seamlessly. The integration also enabled CRED members 
to access travel information including check-in, gate and 
baggage updates within the CRED app. The partnership 
expanded ixigo’s reach among CRED’s premium member 
base and strengthened our distribution of flight services 
through third-party platforms.We partnered with Swiggy to strengthen our Food on 
Train offering, integrating Swiggy’s restaurant network 
into the ixigo Trains and Confirmtkt apps. Travellers 
could order fresh, hygienic meals from 40,000+ trusted 
restaurants across 160+ railway stations, with real-time 
order tracking and delivery directly to their seats. The 
service also offered an assured delivery or full refund 
commitment, adding greater reliability and peace of 
mind to the journey. We launched native apps for ixigo Flights & Hotels, AbhiBus and Confirmtkt 
within ChatGPT, bringing conversational travel discovery, comparison and 
planning across flights, trains, buses and hotels. Travellers can search and 
compare real-time options through natural-language prompts and seamlessly 
proceed to booking through the respective ixigo Group apps and websites. 
The experience supports deep, intent-led queries, including detailed hotel 
preferences, while enabling real-time fare intelligence and nearby-date fare 
comparisons for flights.
We also introduced real-time flight tracking within ChatGPT, providing live status 
and key journey information such as boarding gates, baggage belts and check-
in counters. For trains, Confirmtkt enabled travellers to find confirmed-ticket 
alternatives, while AbhiBus brought conversational bus discovery and comparison 
into the same interface. This extends our distribution beyond owned channels 
while keeping booking and post-booking experiences within our platform.
Annual Report 2025-26 Financial Statements Statutory Reports Corporate Overview

Confirmtkt partnered with Rapido 
to power train bookings within the 
Rapido app, extending its rail booking 
capabilities to users across metros 
and emerging towns. The integration 
brought Confirmtkt’s smart booking 
features, including waitlist prediction 
and alternate travel options, to a 
wider audience, connecting daily 
mobility with long-distance rail 
journeys.AbhiBus partnered with Uber to 
power intercity bus bookings 
within the Uber app, providing 
access to 6 lakh+ routes across

last-mile travel offering.

Tier II and III Cities Remain Our 
Largest Customer Base
Nearly 94% of our transactions either

Tier II and Tier III cities. These markets 
Rising Incomes Reshaping Travel Demand
India's household income profile is steadily shifting towards 
higher discretionary spending. Between 2018 and 2030, the 
number of upper-middle and high-income households is 
projected to increase from 69 million to 197 million, while low-
income households are expected to decline materially.
As household incomes rise, travel behaviour evolves. Travellers 
who once relied primarily on rail increasingly add flights, 
hotels and ancillary services to their journeys, increasing 
both the frequency and diversity of travel and expanding the 
addressable opportunity for our platform.      Technology Landscape
Technology is reshaping how travellers discover, compare, 
book and manage travel. Competitive advantage is shifting 
from inventory access towards understanding traveller 
intent, contextualising information and simplifying decisions. 
AI is accelerating this shift from traditional search towards 
conversational and personalised travel experiences.
Our AI investments reflect this shift, combining proprietary 
travel intelligence, real-time travel data and AI-native products 
to simplify travel discovery, improve decision-making, automate 
workflows and create more contextual customer experiences.

The Opportunity Ahead
The opportunity for our business is supported by three long-term 
structural trends:
A growing 
online travel 
market, with the 
OTA segment 
expanding faster 
than the broader 
travel industry.Rapid digital 
adoption across 
India, bringing

travellers online, 
particularly 
across Tier II and 
Tier III cities.Increasingly 
multimodal 
travel behaviour, 
as consumers 
combine transport, 
accommodation 
and related services 
within their journeys
Together, these trends create a large and expanding opportunity for 
a scaled, technology-led and AI-native travel platform.
Air travel is expected to remain the fastest-
growing segment, while hotels, intercity 
buses and reserved rail travel continue to 
expand in scale. At the same time, India's 
travel ecosystem remains fragmented across 
transport modes, operators and geographies, 
creating an opportunity for platforms that 
can simplify discovery, comparison, booking 
and journey management across categories.
As the market expands, the opportunity is 
not only to serve more travellers, but also 
to participate in a greater share of each 
traveller's journey.are benefiting from expanding transport 
infrastructure, improved road connectivity, 
new airports and wider digital adoption, 
bringing more travellers into online 
travel and increasing the frequency and 
diversity of journeys they undertake. As 
travel demand grows, these markets are 
becoming an important source of the next 
wave of online travel.
Travel in these regions is inherently 
multimodal, with travellers often combining 
trains, buses, flights and accommodation 
within a single itinerary. This creates an 
opportunity to serve multiple travel needs 
through one platform. For the Next Billion 
Users, simplicity, trust and intuitive digital 
experiences can reduce the complexity 
of navigating this fragmented travel 
ecosystem.Travel Is Becoming Multi-Category
Travel planning is increasingly extending 
beyond a single booking, with customers 
comparing transport, accommodation 
and travel protection as part of the same 
journey. A single itinerary can span trains, 
buses, flights, hotels, airport transfers and 
other services, shifting the opportunity 
from individual transactions to serving a 
greater share of the overall journey.
Our business model is built around this 
behaviour. Customers acquired through 
one category can transact across trains, 
flights, buses, hotels and value-added 
services on the same platform. Higher 
cross-category engagement can increase 
customer lifetime value without requiring 
a new customer acquisition for each 
transaction.Business Landscape
India's travel and transportation market is projected to grow from H 3.8 trillion in FY23 to H 5.9 trillion by FY28, creating a significantly larger 
addressable market for our multimodal platform.

through multiple stages of their journey, we gain more opportunities to serve

Each interaction strengthens our understanding of traveller preferences, enabling us to deliver more relevant recommendations,

strengthens the network effects that power our platform.

Management Letter to Shareholders
“At every inflection point, you either disrupt 
yourself, or you get disrupted.
A year ago, in our first Annual Report 
after listing, we wrote about the power 
of compounding. We spoke about 
how years of patient building, difficult 
choices and steady execution can create 
advantages that become stronger over 
time.
FY2025-26 made us think what happens 
when the technology assumptions 
on which an industry is built begin to 
change fundamentally?
Our answer was simple: disrupt 
ourselves first.
FY26 was another strong year for ixigo, 
but it also marked the beginning of a 
deeper reinvention. We chose to rebuild 
parts of the Company around AI and 
rethink how our products, systems and 
teams should work in this new era.
That is why the theme of this Annual 
Report, Travel Reimagined with AI, feels 
especially relevant.
The foundation for this reinvention was 
built over nearly two decades: scale 
across trains, flights and buses; trusted 
consumer brands; proprietary travel data 
and prediction systems; deep supply 
integrations; and hundreds of millions of 
traveller interactions.AI now allows us to combine these 
assets in entirely new ways.
Our purpose, however, remains 
unchanged: to make travel simpler, 
more dependable and more accessible 
for every traveller.
Growing, and Growing Right
FY26 was a strong financial year.
Gross Transaction Value grew 25% year-
on-year to J18,692.7 crore, Revenue 
from Operations increased 34% to 
J1,228.0 crore, and Adjusted EBITDA 
grew 28% to J120.9 crore. Profit After 
Tax stood at J71.5 crore, while Operating 
Cash Flow increased 60% to a record 
J195.7 crore.
What gives us particular satisfaction is 
the quality and breadth of this growth.
Our business is becoming more 
diversified and resilient. In Q4 FY26, Dear Shareholders,
— Made in Fire, Rajnish KumarAnnual Report 2025-26 Financial Statements Statutory Reports Corporate Overview
24 25
=== page 28 ===
flights became our largest vertical 
by GTV, buses became our largest 
contributor to contribution margin, 
while trains remained a highly profitable 
franchise and the volume anchor of our 
ecosystem.
We now have more than one engine of 
growth.
This matters in a market as dynamic as 
travel. When one mode faces capacity 
issues, supply disruptions or policy 
constraints, another one can accelerate. 
Travellers themselves also move across 
these modes. A train traveller may 
take a bus when confirmed tickets 
are unavailable, or a bus traveller may 
become a first-time flyer as incomes rise. 
Someone arriving into a city on a mode 
of transportation may discover how to 
book hotels online for the first time ever. 
India is becoming more multimodal too.
Rising incomes, better highways, new 
airports, Vande Bharat trains, expanding 
bus networks and deeper digital adoption 
are bringing millions of new travellers into 
the organised travel ecosystem. Much of 
this growth is coming from Tier II and Tier 
III India, markets we have been building 
for since long before serving the Next 
Billion Users became fashionable.
Those roots have shaped how we build: 
vernacular-first, utility-led, value-
conscious and obsessed with reducing 
uncertainty.
That brings us to one of the most 
important ways in which our thinking 
about the business has evolved.
We are in the Peace-of-Mind 
Business  
Travel is a high-trust category.
A booking may take a few seconds, but 
its consequences last much longer. A 
delayed train, a cancelled or rescheduled 
flight, an unreliable hotel, an unconfirmed 
ticket or a stuck refund can quickly turn 
excitement into anxiety.
This is why we increasingly see ixigo as 
being in the Peace-of-Mind business.For us, that means using technology to 
reduce uncertainty before, during and 
after the journey.
It means estimating train confirmation 
probabilities before a traveller books. 
Helping a bus passenger stranded after 
a breakdown. Tracking flights in real 
time. Making refunds faster. Finding 
confirmed alternatives. And increasingly 
resolving problems before the traveller 
needs to ask.
In Q4 FY26, our AI systems handled 4.35 
million customer queries end-to-end. 
Autonomous AI agents resolved 81.5% 
of voice conversations and 91.1% of 
chat conversations. Our average refund 
time was approximately 3 hours and 55 
minutes, while 95.7% of customer calls 
were answered within two minutes. 
These are metrics we publish to keep 
ourselves honest about how well we 
are treating our customers and to push 
our teams to set higher standards for 
themselves for the coming quarters. 
Behind every one of these metrics is a 
traveller experiencing less friction.
This philosophy was tested during 
several aviation disruptions of FY26.
Operation Sindoor in May led to 
thousands of flights being cancelled 
or rescheduled and customer contact 
volumes surged. Our teams created a 
dedicated cross-functional response, 
issued proactive updates, shifted 
customers to alternate modes of 
transport enabled rapid refunds and 
alternate options, and made customer-
friendly decisions even when they came 
with a short-term cost.
We saw this again in Q3 with disruptions 
faced by a major carrier in the country. 
Again, we were proactively informing, 
engaging and helping our customers 
with a major role played by AI in this 
crisis. During December alone, AI 
handled more than 150,000 voice calls 
end-to-end. 
And once more in March, with the Iran 
conflict, we saw how painful uncertainty 
can be for travellers and the disruptions 
in the Middle East gave ixigo an opportunity to prove why its empathy 
and customer experience centered 
playbook helps it win trust and high 
ratings. When the going gets tough, the 
tough get going. 
Trust is easiest to talk about when 
everything works. It is earned when 
something goes wrong.
Writing the NEXT Chapter of 
Travel
Artificial intelligence is changing what 
software will mean.
Traditional software asks users to 
understand the product: forms, filters, 
menus and workflows. AI increasingly 
allows the product to understand the 
user.
This is particularly powerful in travel.
A simple journey can involve dozens of 
decisions around price, timing, reliability, 
convenience, inventory and personal 
preference. Much of travel supply is still 
fragmented and unstructured.
When a traveller says, “Find me 
somewhere quiet near the beach for 
three nights with my parents, but not 
too far from the airport,” the underlying 
problem is one of intent.
We believe understanding that intent will 
become increasingly important in travel.
One of our biggest lessons this year is 
that building AI-native products also 
requires the organisation behind them to 
evolve.
We therefore created a focused 
internal effort to rethink our technology 
architecture, making systems AI-
readable, APIs AI-accessible and 
workflows easier for AI agents to use. 
Teams across the Company are also 
changing how products are designed, 
coded, tested and operated.
Development cycles that once took years 
can increasingly be compressed into 
months.
The first major consumer expression of 
this work is ixigo NEXT - an industry-first 
expression for what the AI-Native OTA of 
the Future means. 
Annual Report 2025-26 Financial Statements Statutory Reports Corporate Overview

=== page 29 ===
From Search to Intent to 
Action
ixigo NEXT brings together four 
sources of intelligence: state-of-the-
art language and voice models; deep 
traveller context built over years; 
real-time travel intelligence across 
fares, inventory and disruptions; and 
ixigo’s proprietary systems across 
predictions, recommendations and 
automation.
At the centre of the experience 
is TARA , our multimodal AI travel 
assistant.
TARA understands travellers through 
voice, text and tap across English, 
Hindi and Hinglish.
Travel, however, is also visual and 
decision-heavy. Sometimes a tap is 
better than a conversation. Sometimes 
a map is better than text. Sometimes 
the best experience is for the system 
to quietly handle the task in the 
background.
We therefore see the future travel 
interface as adaptive. It should 
understand what the traveller is 
trying to achieve, surface the right 
information at the right moment and 
stay out of the way when it is not 
needed.
TARA also gives us something 
strategically important: a richer 
understanding of traveller intent.
Historically, digital travel platforms 
could see what users searched, clicked 
and booked.
Conversational interactions add 
another layer. Travellers can tell us why 
they are travelling, who they are with, 
what they care about and what they 
are worried about.
This creates a powerful new feedback 
loop. Better understanding of intent 
leads to more relevant experiences, 
which can improve engagement, trust 
and repeat usage.When AI Starts Doing the 
Heavy Lifting
The next step is agentic execution.
The real promise of AI in travel is that 
travellers should eventually have to 
manage fewer things themselves.
Inside ixigo NEXT, we are developing 
agents for tasks such as automated 
web check-ins, boarding-pass 
retrieval, fare monitoring, hotel 
reconfirmations, refund management, 
booking reconfirmation and proactive 
trip monitoring, with appropriate user 
consent and control.
Imagine getting your boarding pass 
automatically on WhatsApp and in 
your phone wallet as soon as check-in 
opens. Knowing about gate changes 
or baggage belt changes even before 
they are officially announced. Having 
your hotel booking reconfirmed on your 
behalf, with an agent calling the hotel 
for you. Having an agent advocate for 
your rightful refund with suppliers on 
your behalf. Getting waivers on certain 
charges in situations where they matter 
the most.
The mechanics of travel begin to fade 
into the background when agentic AI 
starts pre-emptively doing things for 
you that took several minutes of your 
time away and led to anxiety in the past. 
That is the direction in which we are 
building.
Building a Durable AI 
Advantage
We do not believe access to the latest 
foundation models will, by itself, create 
a durable advantage for any company.
Models will keep improving and 
becoming more widely available.
Our advantage has to come from 
everything around them: proprietary 
traveller context, transaction history, 
travel supply, prediction systems, 
operational workflows, product design, 
distribution and the ability to execute 
reliably at scale.A general model may understand the 
concept of a waitlisted train ticket.
Our systems understand how 
confirmation probability changes, what 
alternatives exist, what products may 
reduce uncertainty and what action the 
traveller may want to take next basis the 
trip’s constraints or their past behaviour. 
We understand which peace of mind 
products need to be layered on top to 
what customer. 
That domain depth matters.
We are also focused on AI economics. 
Different problems require different 
levels of intelligence, speed and cost. We 
are building orchestration systems that 
can choose the right model and tool for 
each task. We are also training our own 
small language models for certain tasks. 
Ultimately, we will judge our AI 
investments by practical outcomes: 
whether we can ship faster, solve more 
customer problems, improve conversion 
and retention, and create greater 
operating leverage.
Long-Term Value Over Short-
Term Optics
Last year, we thanked shareholders for 
supporting a Company that believes in 
long-term value over short-term optics.
FY26 gave that philosophy greater 
meaning.
Our scaled businesses across trains, 
flights and buses are generating 
operating leverage. Where we see large 
opportunities, we are willing to reinvest 
part of that operating leverage.
Today, AI and Hotels are two such areas.
Some investments will show up in costs 
before they show up in productivity or 
revenue. That is inevitable when building 
ahead of a technology transition or 
developing a new category.
Our approach remains disciplined: invest 
where we have conviction, measure 
whether the underlying thesis is working, 
and change course when evidence 
changes.Annual Report 2025-26 Financial Statements Statutory Reports Corporate Overview

=== page 30 ===
By the end of FY27 we will start seeing 
some very early results from these 
investments but both of these will be 
multi-year journeys of transformation 
and vertical building. 
A Year that Tested Travel
FY26 also reminded us why resilience 
matters.
The travel ecosystem faced an unusually 
difficult series of events. The closure 
of Pakistani airspace following the 
Pahalgam attack and Operation Sindoor 
led to flight cancellations and longer 
flight routings. The unfortunate Air India 
accident in June 2025 brought aviation 
safety into sharp focus. December saw 
significant operational disruptions by a 
leading carrier, while conflict in West Asia 
in March brought further uncertainty, 
rerouting and pressure on aviation 
capacity.
Rail also went through important 
changes, including Aadhaar-linked 
authentication in high-demand booking 
windows, user re-verification and 
changes to opening timings of Tatkal for 
OTAs.
For a travel company, these events are 
never just headlines.
A capacity shortage becomes a fare a 
first-time flyer cannot afford. A policy 
change becomes another reason to 
prove our resilience playbook works. 
A cancelled flight becomes a family 
worried about a connection.
Technology becomes valuable when it 
turns this complexity into confidence.
Building the Next Engines of 
Growth
Our portfolio today has distinct but 
complementary roles.
Buses have become our largest 
contributor to contribution margin and 
one of our fastest-growing verticals. 
FY26 bus GTV grew 46% and revenue 
grew 51%. AbhiBus continues to 
deepen its supply footprint, including 
government bus operators, while products such as roadside assistance 
address some of the category’s biggest 
trust problems. 
Flights have become our largest 
business by GTV and remain an 
important growth engine which can 
help us penetrate deeper into Tier-1 
markets where we still have a lot of users 
who may not use us yet. During FY26, 
flight GTV grew 33% and revenue grew 
54%, despite a challenging aviation 
environment. 
We are also expanding beyond consumer 
distribution through platforms such 
as BusBiz and our AI-first operating 
platform, busGDS.ai.
There is still plenty of road ahead for 
buses (pun intended) and we will keep 
making our customer experience in the 
space stand out as top notch. 
Trains remain the foundation of our 
Next Billion User ecosystem, a large 
and profitable franchise and one of 
our strongest channels for efficient 
customer acquisition. Their strategic 
value extends well beyond train tickets. 
Train users increasingly discover buses, 
hotels, food and other travel services 
across our ecosystem.
That is multimodal compounding in 
action.
Hotels: Learn First, Then Scale
Hotels are the new emerging vertical 
that is already our fastest-growing line 
of business and presents unprecedented 
opportunity in a segment that is 
underpenetrated in terms of online 
bookings. 
India’s mid-market and budget hotel 
segment still has a meaningful trust gap 
between what a traveller sees online and 
what they may experience on arrival.
We believe this creates an opportunity 
to bring our peace-of-mind philosophy 
into accommodation.
Since the end of FY26, this strategy has 
accelerated.Through HELLO, our AI-first hotel 
partner platform, we are onboarding 
several thousand properties every 
quarter and have built direct 
relationships with more than 10,000 
hotels across nearly 700 towns.
Our acquisition of a majority stake in 
Brevistay, completed after the end of 
FY26, adds direct hotel relationships 
and flexible-stay expertise, while ixigo 
brings technology, distribution and a 
large captive traveller base.
We will build more direct supply and 
we believe it should achieve product-
market fit by the end of FY27 and grow 
rapidly thereafter. 
Hotels will take time to scale. But over 
the next four to five years, our ambition 
is clear: to become India’s leading 
discovery and booking platforms for the 
mid-market and budget hotel traveller.
Taking Our Travel Technology 
Beyond India
FY26 also marked an important 
international milestone.
We acquired a majority stake in Trenes.
com, the second-largest train OTA in 
Spain.
Europe is one of the world’s most 
developed rail markets. Trenes brings 
deep local integrations and market 
expertise, while ixigo brings nearly 
two decades of travel technology and 
experience solving complex rail journeys 
at scale.
We see this as an opportunity to cross-
pollinate product and AI capabilities 
between India and Europe.
Building Strategic 
Optionalities
In FY26 and the months before 
publishing this annual report, we have 
also made certain minority investments 
in AI-native companies. 
We view these as a way to stay 
close to frontier AI talent, accelerate Annual Report 2025-26 Financial Statements Statutory Reports Corporate Overview

experimentation and build strategic

We are also expanding surfaces where

The People Behind the 
Transformation
Every number in this letter is ultimately 
the work of ixigems who build, ship,

As AI becomes more capable, we 
believe human qualities such as 
judgment, taste, empathy, agency,

become even more important.AI can generate possibilities. Judgment

AI can automate workflows. Empathy

And when similar tools are available 
to everyone, agency and ambition

Our goal is to use technology as a force

What We Are Building Towards
India sits at a remarkable convergence 
point.
Travel infrastructure is expanding. 
Household incomes are rising. More 
Indians are taking their first flight, first 
inter-city bus journey or first leisure trip. 
Digital adoption continues to deepen.
At the same time, AI is changing the 
relationship between people and 
software.
We believe travel will be one of the 
biggest beneficiaries.
The next phase of ixigo will be shaped 
by hyper-personalisation, intelligent 
automation, stronger post-book 
experiences, direct hotel supply, deeper 
multimodal cross-sell, international 
rail and an intelligence layer that 
understands more of the traveller’s 
context.
Our ambition is to make the mechanics 
of travel increasingly invisible and reduce 
the moments of anxiety to near zero. 
The traveller should spend less time 
comparing endless options, tracking 
changes, making calls, chasing refunds 
and worrying about what might go 
wrong.
And more time travelling, discovering 
and connecting to people and places.We are building for the next 500 
million Indians entering the travel 
economy, many of whom will experience 
categories such as air travel and online 
hotels for the first time through a mobile 
phone.
Technology should make that world 
feel simple.
Looking Ahead, Together
We end FY26 grateful for what has 
compounded behind us and excited 
about what lies ahead.
The AI transition will move quickly. 
Models will change, costs will fall, 
interfaces will evolve and some of 
today’s assumptions will prove wrong.
Our job is to keep learning, stay 
disciplined in how we allocate capital, 
remain close to the traveller and be 
willing to reinvent ourselves when the 
world changes.
That spirit has been part of ixigo from 
the beginning.
To our customers, thank you for trusting 
us with journeys that matter.
To our partners, thank you for continuing 
to build with us.To our shareholders, thank you for 
supporting our long-term vision and 
giving us the confidence to invest 
boldly while remaining disciplined about 
value creation.

Train Business
Trains remains the foundation of our platform and our largest source of customer engagement. During 
FY26, passenger segments crossed 104.12 million, while Train GTV increased to J82,789.49 million.
GTV (J Mn) 82,789.49FY26
Revenue (J Mn) 5,112.57
Passenger Segments (Mn) 104.12
Contribution Margin 30.42%The Trains industry experienced several structural 
and regulatory changes during FY26, including a 
longer restricted window for OTAs and agents for 
Tatkal bookings, reduced waitlist inventory, tighter 
authentication measures and mandatory Aadhaar 
verification. These changes affected the broader rail and 
OTA ecosystem, temporarily altering booking patterns and 
limiting inventory availability across parts of the market, 
against a high base of elevated demand seen during the 
Maha Kumbh period in FY25.     
Extended Tatkal access windows for OTAs, a lower 
waitlist ticket pool and mandatory Aadhaar verification 
moderated industry growth following the elevated Maha 
Kumbh demand seen in FY25.     Industry-wide regulatory changes

discovery, booking and the overall journey, including AI

=== page 35 ===
Flight Business
Flights emerged as our largest business by Gross Transaction Value during Q4 FY26, reflecting the growing role of air 
travel within our multimodal platform. Over the last three years, Flight GTV has more than doubled, supported by rising 
domestic travel and growing demand from international routes.
Flight Revenue increased by 54.18% to H3,906.78 
million, despite a muted domestic aviation market and a 
challenging international travel environment during the 
year. In Q4 FY26, the domestic flights market grew only 
1.3% YoY, while our Flight passenger segments grew 
nearly 14%, reflecting continued market share gains.
We continued to expand international supply 
through global GDS partnerships with Amadeus and 
Travelport (Galileo), alongside 26 airline integrations 
through NDC channels. International Flight GTV grew 
substantially in FY26.      Outperforming the market
Expanding International TravelWe continued to strengthen the flight experience 
through products designed to improve choice, flexibility, 
convenience and peace of mind. The enhanced Price Lock 
product enabled travellers to lock airfares for up to 14 days, 
while AI Smart Filters on desktop allowed users to search 
and refine flight options using natural language. We also 
expanded the flight journey into first- and last-mile mobility 
through the launch of Airport Cabs, and introduced Armed 
Forces Fares for serving and retired defence personnel and 
their dependents.Product and customer experience innovationFY26 Developments

Bus Business
Bus GTV increased by 45.50% to J26,207.47 million, 
while passenger segments grew by 44.26% to 26.66 
million. Growth was supported by strong demand across 
intercity routes, with pilgrimage travel emerging as 
a notable demand driver during the year, particularly 
across destinations such as Tirupati, Nashik, Ujjain, 
Varanasi and Haridwar.
AbhiBus expanded its State Road Transport Corporation 
(SRTC) network to 22 major state-run bus operators 
across India, adding eight new partnerships during the 
year, including KSRTC, KKRTC, NWKRTC, SBSTC, 
OSRTC, UTC, MTC and CTC. This significantly 
broadened government bus inventory 
and enabled inter-city and inter-state 
bookings across most major regions of 
India. We also expanded distribution 
by powering intercity bus bookings 
on Uber and launching the AbhiBus 
native app on ChatGPT.Fastest-growing segment
Expanding supply and distribution     We continued to invest across both sides of the bus 
ecosystem. Our ReadyGo platform provides bus 
operators with real-time operational insights, while 
our industry-first Roadside Assistance programme 
offers passengers assured cab support in the event 
of bus breakdowns or incidents. Together, these 
initiatives strengthened operational efficiency and 
traveller confidence beyond the booking experienceProduct and technology innovation FY26 DevelopmentsBoard’s Report

Over the past five years, we have reduced our average refund turnaround time through continued investment in AI,  
automation and service capabilities. Today, our service infrastructure is designed to support customers across every stage of their 
journey, from booking and preparation to disruption management and post-trip resolution.  
 
Our Peace of Mind portfolio extends beyond booking to support customers before, during and after their journey. Products such 
as Assured, Assured Flex, Price Lock, Abhi Assured, Alternate Travel Plan and Travel Guarantee address refund uncertainty, itinerary 
changes and travel disruptions, allowing travellers to choose the level of protection and flexibility that best suits their needs.We Are in the “Peace of Mind” Business
Reducing Anxiety for Travellers
Travel is inherently uncertain. Flights can be delayed, plans can change, 
bookings can remain waitlisted and journeys can be disrupted. We 
believe our role extends beyond enabling transactions to helping 
travellers navigate these moments with greater confidence.
Service Performance
Average refund time AI-handled queries Calls answered within  
2 minutesAncillary attachment rate 3h 16m 12.71 Mn 96.50% 29.10%
AI is increasingly embedded across voice, chat and post-booking support, enabling faster resolution, proactive assistance 
and greater automation of routine interactions. This allows our service teams to focus on more complex travel requirements 
while helping us deliver consistent support as transaction volumes scale.
During the December 2025 peak travel season, widespread flight disruptions led to a sharp increase in customer queries, 
refunds and rebooking requests. Our AI-powered service infrastructure scaled to meet the surge, handling more than 
150,000 voice calls end-to-end during the month. This demonstrated the ability of our AI systems to augment customer 
support at scale and maintain service responsiveness during periods of exceptional demand.AI-enabled Service Delivery

=== page 39 ===
Customer grievances are managed through a process integrated with our customer support platform. 
Complaints received through chat, voice, email or physical correspondence are acknowledged, assigned a 
unique ticket number and tracked through to closure. This allows customers to monitor the status of their 
request while enabling our teams to manage escalations consistently across channels.
Our grievance framework targets acknowledgement within 24 hours and resolution within 15 working 
days, subject to the availability of the information required to investigate the request. Where additional 
information is needed or the matter requires coordination with travel partners, customers are kept informed 
until the case is resolved.Customer Grievance Redressal

=== page 40 ===
Building Greater Confidence and 
Flexibility into Every Journey
These services complement our core travel offerings across trains, flights, buses and hotels, helping us enhance the customer 
experience while creating additional value across the travel journey. Together, they form an important part of ixigo’s broader 
ancillary ecosystem and help deepen customer engagement beyond the core booking.Travel can come with uncertainty - from fluctuating fares and waitlisted tickets 
to changing plans and unexpected disruptions. At ixigo, we are building a 
growing suite of Peace of Mind offerings that help travellers navigate these 
uncertainties with greater confidence, flexibility and protection.
ixigo Assured & Assured Flex
These services offer travellers greater confidence 
and flexibility while booking. Assured provides full 
refunds with no questions asked, while Assured 
Flex allows travellers to reschedule their bookings 
without penalties.Price Lock
New & improved Price Lock gives travellers greater control 
and confidence while booking flights, allowing them to lock 
airfares for up to 14 days and protect against fare increases 
of up to H8,000. This gives travellers more time to finalise 
their plans while securing the fare they choose.
Alternate Travel Plan
Offers up to 3X refund on unconfirmed waitlisted 
train tickets, giving travellers greater confidence 
when making last-minute bookings or planning 
journeys during periods of high demandAbhi Assured
A service quality assurance programme that, for a nominal 
price, offers travellers up to 150% assured refund in case 
of bus delays or cancellations and compensation for a poor 
travel experience. Together, these benefits help ensure a safe, 
comfortable and seamless travel experience for bus travellers.
Roadside Assistance
An industry-first feature by AbhiBus, Roadside 
Assistance supports passengers in the event of a 
bus breakdown during their journey, addressing a 
key concern associated with intercity bus travel. 
In case of a bus breakdown, an alternative cab is 
arranged within one hour at no additional cost to 
travellers, enabling passengers to continue their 
journey with minimal disruptionTravel Guarantee
Offers travellers up to 3X refund on bus tickets if 
cancelled by the bus operator, providing greater 
assurance and protection against unexpected 
changes to their travel plans.

Network for Digital Commerce (ONDC) to

with in-app payments. The integration 
expanded ixigo’s multimodal journey offering,

buses, flights and hotels through a single 
platform. It also extended ixigo Trains beyond 
intercity rail into everyday urban mobility, 
strengthening its role in connecting first- and 
last-mile journeys with longer-distance travel.     
ixigo renewed our partnership with Rohit Sharma as

Risk Management
Confidence Through Foresight
We have established an enterprise-wide Risk Management Framework to 
identify, assess, monitor and manage risks that may influence our strategy, 
operations and financial performance.
Risk management is embedded within our governance, strategic planning and operational processes, enabling informed 
decision-making and proactive mitigation as our business evolves.
Oversight rests with our Board of Directors through the Risk Management Committee, which reviews the enterprise risk 
profile, emerging risks and mitigation strategies. Management is responsible for implementing the framework throughout the 
organisation, while designated risk owners monitor and manage risks within their respective areas of responsibility.
Board  
of DirectorsRisk Management 
Committee ManagementBusiness  
Functions
	 Provides overall 
oversight of the 
enterprise risk 
management 
framework. 	 Reviews the enterprise 
risk profile. 
	 Monitors key risks and 
mitigation plans. 
	 Reports significant 
matters to the Board. 	 Identifies, assesses and 
manages risks across 
business functions. 
	 Implements mitigation 
plans and monitors 
emerging risks. 	 Own operational risks. 
	 Report changes in risk 
exposure and control 
effectiveness. 
Risk Assessment Framework
Risks are assessed using a structured framework 
that considers both the likelihood of occurrence 
and the potential business impact. The assessment 
supports prioritisation of mitigation actions, 
allocation of responsibilities and periodic review 
by management and the Risk Management 
Committee.

Management Discussion and Analysis
Economy 
Global economy 
In CY 2026, the global economy recorded growth of 3.3%, 
while Emerging Market and Developing Economies (EMDEs) 
continued to maintain stable momentum. Growth across 
several EMDEs was supported by stronger net exports and 
higher investment activity. Advanced economies expanded by 
1.9%, while global inflation stood at 4.1%. During the year, global 
trade policies increasingly reflected geopolitical and security 
priorities over efficiency considerations and established 
multilateral frameworks. Additionally, the outbreak of war in 
the Middle East created further pressure on global economic 
activity. The resulting closure of the Strait of Hormuz, along 
with significant damage to critical production facilities, led to 
an energy crisis of unprecedented scale. Inflation is expected 
to rise to 4.4% in CY 2026 and thereafter resume its downward 
trajectory in the following year. However, risks arising from 
increasing geopolitical tensions and potential disruptions in 
global trade continue to persist. In this environment, timely 
and coordinated policy actions by governments will remain 
critical to address the evolving macroeconomic challenges in 
the period ahead.1

Indian economy
Despite multiple global headwinds, rising uncertainty in global 
trade and the imposition of high penal tariffs, the Government 
of India responded with calibrated policy measures and viewed these challenges as an opportunity to strengthen the domestic 
economy. Key measures included GST rationalisation, faster 
deregulation and simplification of compliance requirements 
across sectors. The Government also continued to introduce 
timely policy interventions to strengthen domestic growth 
drivers, diversify exports, improve resilience against global 
shocks and manage external sector risks.
In FY 2026, the Indian economy recorded real GDP growth of 
7.7%, reflecting strong underlying momentum in economic 
activity.2 Growth was supported by robust consumption and 
investment trends, aided by favourable financial conditions, 
supportive policy measures and ongoing structural reforms. The 
resilience in demand was equally visible on the supply side of 
the economy. Manufacturing activity witnessed improvement 
during the year, while the services sector continued to remain a 
major contributor to overall economic growth.
India’s manufacturing sector is gradually undergoing 
transformation, supported by policy direction and industrial 
expansion. The Production Linked Incentive (PLI) scheme 
remains central to this transition. With an incentive outlay of 
₹1.97 lakh crore and approvals across 14 strategic sectors, the 
scheme has evolved beyond being only a financial support 
mechanism.3 On the external financing front, gross Foreign 
Direct Investment (FDI) recorded strong growth, while net FDI 
also improved during the year. India continued to remain an 
attractive destination for greenfield FDI projects.
Going forward, India is expected to maintain its growth 
momentum, with GDP projected to grow by 6.6%. 4The Union 
Budget 2026–27 introduced the framework of ‘Kartavyas’, with 
a strong emphasis on accelerating economic growth. FY 2027 
is expected to witness a phase of economic adjustment as 
businesses and households adapt to evolving conditions, 
while demand and investment activity continue to strengthen. 
Further, the Government’s continued focus on expanding 
domestic manufacturing across strategic and frontier sectors 
is expected to support India’s long-term growth trajectory. 
On the demand side, private consumption in FY 2026–27 is 
expected to remain supported by discretionary spending. Rural 
demand is expected to stay resilient, while urban consumption 
is likely to strengthen further, supported by the positive 
impact of GST rationalisation and sustained momentum in the 
services sector.

Travel & Tourism Industry
Global industry 
The global travel and tourism sector continued to demonstrate 
steady growth during the year. The industry was supported by 
increasing investments in transportation infrastructure, ongoing 
digital transformation and the expanding middle-class traveller 
base. Growing cultural and leisure aspirations of travellers across 
the world also contributed to market growth. Leisure travel 
continued to lead the market, driven by its broader and more 
experience-oriented nature. At the same time, business travel 
remained an essential component of international networking 
and economic activity. The segment is also witnessing structural 
changes led by globalisation and increasing cross-border 
partnerships. Educational tourism continued to emerge as an 
important contributor to the market, combining learning and 
travel while generating both economic and cultural benefits. At 
the regional level, Europe remained the largest market, supported 
by a strong concentration of globally recognised destinations 
and well-established tourism infrastructure.
The global travel and tourism industry continued to remain 
dynamic, facilitating the movement of people for recreational, 
commercial, educational and medical purposes. The sector 
plays an important role in connecting travellers with cultural, 
recreational and natural destinations, thereby contributing 
significantly to global economic development. During the 
reporting year, industry participants continued to offer 
attractive tourism services and customised travel solutions for 
corporates, supporting overall market growth. 
The global travel and tourism market is projected to grow at a 
CAGR of 8.0% between 2025 and 2035 and is expected to reach 
US$16,310.1 billion by CY 2035.5 Overall, the global travel and 
tourism industry remains diverse, adaptable and continuously 
evolving. The increasing adoption of digital tools and platforms is improving convenience, enabling personalised services and 
enhancing travel experiences across regions and customer 
segments. Urban development, eco-tourism initiatives and 
heritage restoration are also becoming important focus areas 
for countries seeking to strengthen cultural and experiential 
tourism offerings. In addition, the medical tourism segment is 
expected to dominate the market during the forecast period, 
supported by the rising prevalence of chronic diseases globally.
Indian Industry
India’s tourism sector continues to represent a strong growth 
opportunity, supported by the country’s rich cultural heritage 
and diverse natural attractions. The sector witnessed significant 
progress in 2026, driven by robust economic growth, rising 
middle-class income levels, increasing technology adoption 
and evolving traveller preferences. Tourism has emerged 
as a key pillar of the Indian economy, with strong potential 
for employment generation, foreign exchange earnings 
and balanced regional development. The sector also has a 
significant multiplier effect across hospitality, transportation, 
handicrafts and allied industries, making it an important driver 
of economic growth.
With its cultural diversity, heritage assets and wide range 
of destinations, India is increasingly becoming a preferred 
destination in the global tourism landscape. Beyond its economic 
contribution, the sector also plays an important role in improving 
quality of life through large-scale employment generation. 
While international tourist arrivals continue to remain important, 
domestic tourism continues to play a critical role in supporting 
sector growth. India ranks 8th  globally in terms of tourism 
economy size, contributing USD 231.6 billion and improving 
from its earlier 10th  position.6 The sector contributes 5.22% to 
India’s GDP on a total impact basis, with a direct contribution of 
2.72%. It also supports 13.34% of total employment, including 
5.82% through direct employment, highlighting its importance 
in livelihood creation and inclusive economic development.7

Going forward, India’s domestic tourism market is expected to 
become an even larger contributor to overall sector growth. At 
the same time, continued focus on attracting foreign tourists 
is expected to support foreign exchange earnings. By 2027, 
India is projected to become the fifth-largest outbound tourism 
market, improving from the tenth position in 2019.8 Further, 
by 2030, the sector is expected to generate nearly $30.5 
billion in foreign exchange earnings and attract around 25 
million foreign tourist arrivals.9 With continued policy support, 
sustained investments and increasing focus on both domestic 
and international tourism, India’s tourism sector is expected 
to continue contributing meaningfully to economic growth, 
employment generation and cultural exchange.
Online Travel Agent Market
Global 
Online Travel Agencies (OTAs) continued to play a critical role 
in the hospitality industry by serving as both marketing and 
distribution platforms. They enabled hotels to connect with a 
large base of travellers who increasingly preferred digital booking 
channels. OTAs offered convenience, wider choice, competitive 
pricing and secure payment systems, making them important 
partners for hotels despite the associated commission costs. 
The global hospitality industry continued its transition towards 
online booking channels, with OTAs accounting for a significant 
share of digital travel reservations. Travellers increasingly 
relied on digital platforms for trip discovery, booking and 
travel management. Mobile-first interfaces continued to gain 
preference over desktop platforms. By 2025, mobile platforms 
accounted for a significant share of transactional traffic, 
supported by features such as biometric logins, offline itinerary 
access and real-time travel alerts.
The Asia-Pacific region emerged as a leading market for app-
based travel bookings. Transportation continued to account 
for the highest booking volumes, while the accommodation 
segment witnessed rapid growth due to the increasing 
popularity of alternative stays, which also supported inventory 
expansion and improved margins. These trends continued to 
shape the strategies adopted by players in the online travel 
agency market, alongside investments in app infrastructure and 
embedded payment solutions.
The growing adoption of mobile usage transformed the travel 
ecosystem and encouraged travel companies to prioritise 
mobile-first customer experiences. Rising disposable income 
among the expanding middle-class population also enabled 
a larger number of consumers to undertake leisure travel that 
was previously less accessible. This trend increased demand 
for online bookings across flights, accommodations and 
experiential travel offerings, thereby supporting revenue growth 
for travel platforms globally. More than 63% of digitally active 
travellers compared prices across at least two platforms before 
confirming bookings, highlighting the importance of OTAs for 
B2B partners. Promotional campaigns influenced nearly 27% of international trips, while around 22% of travellers booked 
experiences only after evaluating at least five OTA offerings.
The United States remained a key market for OTA adoption, 
with nearly 89% of leisure travellers using digital platforms for 
travel bookings. The OTA industry continued to evolve rapidly, 
supported by technological advancements and changing 
consumer preferences. Travellers increasingly preferred 
convenience and personalised travel experiences, prompting 
online platforms to enhance customised itinerary offerings 
and integrated travel solutions. Growth during the period was 
supported by rising digital tourism engagement, increasing 
preference for flexible booking options, higher dependence on 
online travel ecosystems and growing demand for convenience-
led travel planning. Key industry trends included the increasing 
adoption of online travel booking platforms, growing use of 
internet-based travel services and rising reliance on virtual 
travel assistance solutions.
The OTA market is expected to witness strong growth as 
digitalisation, convenience and changing consumer travel 
behaviour continue to reshape the global tourism landscape. To 
remain competitive, OTAs are expanding value-added offerings 
such as book-now-pay-later options, loyalty programmes and 
bundled travel packages. Emerging technologies, including 
voice search, virtual assistants and augmented reality-enabled 
virtual property tours, are expected to further enhance user 
experience. The global OTA industry is projected to increase 
from USD 561.30 billion in CY 2026 to USD 761.33 billion by 
CY 2031, growing at a CAGR of 6.29%. Leading online booking 
platforms are increasingly adopting advanced technology 
systems to improve efficiency and meet evolving traveller 
expectations. As travellers continue to seek convenience and 
personalised experiences, online platforms are expected to 
further strengthen integrated travel solutions and customised 
service offerings, thereby supporting the continued growth of 
online travel services globally.
Indian Industry 
The Indian online travel market witnessed strong growth in 
recent years, with market size increasing to USD 23.34 billion 
in 2025. Growth was supported by broad-based recovery 
across air travel, hotels and bus services, along with increasing 
demand for curated holiday packages among middle-income 
households. Travellers increasingly preferred immersive travel 
experiences rather than focusing only on transportation and 
accommodation. The adoption of digital payments, coupled with 
rising internet penetration and mobile usage, reduced friction in 
the booking process and improved conversion of online searches 
into confirmed bookings across flights, railways, buses, hotels 
and holiday packages. Improved regional air connectivity under 
the UDAN scheme also expanded access to newer routes. This 
enabled OTAs and supplier-direct platforms to promote these 
destinations through targeted campaigns, resulting in increased 
use of digital travel channels beyond major metropolitan cities.

Filings by leading OTAs reflected consistent growth in gross

in AI-powered assistants and automated customer support

Online travel agencies accounted for 81.74% of total bookings, 
underlining their dominant position in digital travel distribution. 
Geographically, North India accounted for 33.73% of the online 
travel market in 2025, supported by a well-established mix of 
heritage, spiritual and urban tourism destinations. Strong air, 
rail and highway connectivity also enabled seamless online-to-
offline travel experiences. West India is expected to witness 
strong growth, with the market projected to expand at a CAGR 
of 13.35% through 2031, supported by the growth of corporate 
hubs and coastal tourism destinations that continue to diversify 
travel offerings.
The Indian online travel market continued to feature large 
integrated OTAs alongside strong supplier-direct channels 
across air, rail and hotel segments. The competitive landscape 
remained shaped by product depth, loyalty programmes and 
service reliability. Leading OTAs continued to report growth 
in gross bookings across air, hotel and bus categories, while 
investments in AI-enabled assistants and app enhancements 
helped improve customer engagement and lower transaction 
servicing costs.
The India online travel market is projected to grow from USD 
23.34 billion in 2025 to USD 25.38 billion in 2026 and further 
reach USD 38.58 billion by 2031, registering a CAGR of 8.74% 
during the 2026–2031 period. The growth outlook is expected 
to remain supported by continued expansion across air, hotel 
and bus bookings, increasing demand for curated travel 
experiences and technology-led improvements in convenience 
and service quality. Going forward, the market is expected to 
remain highly competitive, led by integrated OTAs and supplier-
direct platforms. Increased adoption of AI-driven solutions, app 
enhancements and targeted promotional strategies is expected 
to further strengthen customer engagement and optimise 
servicing efficiency.10
India’s Aviation Industry
India’s aviation sector has emerged as one of the fastest-
growing segments of the economy. Its contribution extends 
beyond air transportation services to sectors such as tourism, 
trade, logistics, manufacturing and employment generation. 
Given the country’s geographical scale and cultural diversity, 
air connectivity has become an important driver of economic 
integration and national development. The sector plays a 
significant role in supporting business expansion, attracting 
investments and improving access to markets and capital. It 
also facilitates knowledge exchange and innovation across 
industries. The economic impact of aviation extends well 
beyond airlines and airports. Investments in the sector generate 
a strong multiplier effect, with every rupee spent contributing 
more than three times its value in economic activity. The sector also supports employment generation across interconnected 
industries. In FY 2026, the aviation sector supported more 
than 7.7 million indirect jobs, along with nearly 369,000 direct 
jobs across aviation-related operations. India has already 
established itself as the world’s third-largest domestic aviation 
market and continues to witness rapid expansion.11 The number 
of operational airports increased significantly, reflecting the 
Government’s sustained focus on infrastructure development 
and regional connectivity. Air travel has also evolved into a mode 
of mass mobility across the country. During the reporting year, 
commercial aviation accounted for 85.12% of the Indian aviation 
market. Growth was driven by aggressive expansion by both 
full-service and low-cost carriers through larger fleet additions 
and higher route frequencies. This expansion also resulted in 
a significant increase in passenger traffic across domestic 
and international routes. India’s aviation industry currently 
stands at an important phase marked by both opportunities 
and operational challenges. Rising passenger traffic, airport 
modernisation, expansion in regional aviation and increasing 
industrial ambitions continue to reshape the sector. At the same 
time, volatility in fuel prices, taxation-related issues, supply-
chain disruptions and growing safety expectations continue to 
test the resilience of airlines and other aviation stakeholders.
The next decade is expected to determine whether India 
remains primarily a fast-growing aviation market or evolves 
into a comprehensive aviation ecosystem with capabilities 
across aircraft manufacturing, maintenance, repair and overhaul 
(MRO), technology development and stronger regulatory 
governance. Industry estimates indicate that the Indian aviation 
market, valued at USD 16.53 billion in 2026, is projected to grow 
to USD 28.96 billion by 2031, registering a CAGR of 11.86%.12 The 
Government’s long-term vision for the aviation sector is aligned 
with India’s centenary year of independence in 2047. Under this 
vision, the number of airports across the country is expected 
to increase to nearly 350–400 by 2047. Passenger traffic is 
projected to increase almost six-fold by 2040 and reach nearly 
1.1 billion passengers annually. Similarly, India’s commercial 
airline fleet is expected to expand significantly from nearly 400 
aircraft in 2014 to around 2,359 aircraft by March 2040.13  
The domestic industry continues to face near-term operational 
pressures. One of the key concerns for Indian airlines remains 
volatility in crude oil prices, particularly in the context of 
geopolitical tensions in West Asia. Fluctuations in aviation 
turbine fuel (ATF) prices continue to directly impact airline 
profitability, ticket pricing and route sustainability. In response, 
the Government has adopted a balanced and calibrated 
approach instead of direct market intervention. Policy measures 
are increasingly focused on long-term structural improvements, 
including better fuel efficiency, fleet modernisation, adoption 
of Sustainable Aviation Fuel (SAF) and rationalisation of state-
level taxes on ATF. While certain support measures have been 
extended for domestic operations, international routes continue 
to remain largely market-driven.
